Exchange-traded funds and open-ended mutual funds are considered a single population for comparative purposes. It is calculated based on a Morningstar Risk-Adjusted Return measure that accounts for variation in a managed product’s monthly excess performance, placing more emphasis on downward variations and rewarding consistent performance. The Morningstar Rating does not include any adjustment for sales load. The top 10% of products in each product category receive 5 stars, the next 22.5% receive 4 stars, the next 35% receive 3 stars, the next 22.5% receive 2 stars, and the bottom 10% receive 1 star. The Overall Morningstar Rating for a managed product is derived from a weighted average of the performance figures associated with its three-, five-, and 10-year (if applicable) Morningstar Rating metrics. The weights are: 100% three- year rating for 36-59 months of total returns, 60% five-year rating/40% three-year rating for 60-119 months of total returns, and 50% 10-year rating/30% five-year rating/20% three-year rating for 120 or more months of total returns. While the 10-year overall star rating formula seems to give the most weight to the 10-year period, the most recent three-year period actually has the greatest impact because it is included in all three rating periods.69:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"text-background not-last:mb-6\",\"children\":[[\"$84\"]]}]\n6a:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"text-background not-last:mb-6\",\"children\":[[\"As of 9/30, XYLD was rated against the following numbers of Derivative Income funds over the following time periods: 100 during the last 3 years, 69 during the last 5 years, 40 during the last 10 years. An option is a contract sold by one party to another that gives the buyer the right, but not the obligation, to buy (call) or sell (put) a stock at an agreed upon price within a certain period or on a specific date. A covered call option involves holding a long position in a particular asset and writing a call option on that same asset with the goal of realizing additional income from the option premium. By selling covered call options, the fund limits its opportunity to profit from an increase in the price of the underlying index above the exercise price, but continues to bear the risk of a decline in the index. A liquid market may not exist for options held by the fund. While the fund receives premiums for writing the call options, the price it realizes from the exercise of an option could be substantially below the index’s current market price.\"]]}]\n70:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"text-background not-last:mb-6\",\"children\":[[\"Shares of ETFs are bought and sold at market price (not NAV) and are not individually redeemed from the Fund. id="fund-overview" class="w-full bg-foreground gutter-x py-12 lg:py-16"><div class="container-content"><div class="flex flex-col gap-2 pb-3"><div class="h-1.5 w-6 bg-bright-accent" aria-hidden="true"></div><h2 class="font-heading text-4xl font-medium leading-tight sm:text-5xl lg:text-6xl text-background">Overview</h2></div><div class="mt-6 grid grid-cols-1 gap-12 lg:gap-6 lg:grid-cols-2"><div class="flex flex-col gap-6"><div class="flex flex-col gap-2"><h3 class="font-heading text-2xl font-medium leading-tight sm:text-3xl lg:text-4xl text-background">About This Fund</h3><div class="text-base text-muted-foreground leading-6"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The Global X S&amp;P 500® Covered Call ETF (XYLD) follows a “covered call” or “buy-write” strategy, in which the Fund buys stocks in the S&amp;P 500 Index, or investments with economic characteristics similar to those of the Index’s component securities (either individually or in aggregate), and “writes” or “sells” corresponding call options on the same index.</p><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">Distributions may contain return of capital.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-6"><div class="flex flex-col gap-2"><h3 class="font-heading text-2xl font-medium leading-tight sm:text-3xl lg:text-4xl text-background">How the Fund Works</h3><div class="text-base text-muted-foreground leading-6"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The Global X S&amp;P 500® Covered Call ETF (XYLD) seeks to provide investment results that correspond generally to the price and yield performance, before fees and expenses, of the Cboe S&amp;P 500 BuyWrite Index. </p><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The Index is designed to track the performance of a hypothetical covered call (buy-write) strategy applied to the S&amp;P 500 Index, which measures the total return of 500 leading companies listed in the U.S. The Index “holds” the constituents of the S&amp;P 500 Index and “writes” call options on the same index, with the long (held) equity component and the short (sold) call option component held in equal notional amounts, hypothetically generating option premiums that compound. The fund seeks to make monthly distributions.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-4"><p class="text-base text-background font-semibold">Highlights</p><ul class="grid grid-cols-1 gap-4 sm:grid-cols-2"><li class="flex items-start gap-2"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" id="S:37"></div><script>$RS("S:37","P:37")</script><div hidden id="S:38"><div class="flex flex-col gap-6"><div class="flex flex-col gap-1"><p class="font-bold text-xs text-muted-foreground">What is Premium/Discount Chart?</p><div class="max-w-6xl text-xs text-muted-foreground font-sans [font-weight:var(--type-footnote-font-weight)] [&amp;_p]:text-muted-foreground"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The Premium/Discount chart demonstrates the difference between the daily market price of shares of the Fund and the Fund&#x27;s net asset value (NAV). The vertical axis of the chart shows the premium or discount of the daily market price as a percentage of the NAV. The horizontal axis shows each trading day in the time period, and each data point in the chart represents the Premium/Discount on that day. The data presented in the chart and table above represent past performance and cannot be used to predict future results.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-1"><p class="font-bold text-xs text-muted-foreground">How can the Fund trade at a premium/discount to its NAV?</p><div class="max-w-6xl text-xs text-muted-foreground font-sans [font-weight:var(--type-footnote-font-weight)] [&amp;_p]:text-muted-foreground"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The primary explanation is that timing discrepancies can arise between the NAV and the trading price of the Fund. Since shares of the Fund trade on the open market, prices are affected by the constant flow of information received by investors, corporations and financial institutions. Depending on how this changing information affects investor sentiment, shares of the Fund may deviate slightly from the value of the Fund&#x27;s underlying assets. The NAV of the Fund is only calculated once a day (normally at 4:00 p.m. eastern time). As a result, shareholders may pay more than NAV when they buy Fund shares and receive less than NAV when they sell those shares, because shares are purchased and sold at current market prices. However, due to the creation and redemption process that is unique to ETFs, market makers are able to minimize these deviations from NAV by taking advantage of arbitrage opportunities.</p></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-1"><p class="font-bold text-xs text-muted-foreground">What causes these time discrepancies?</p><div class="max-w-6xl text-xs text-muted-foreground font-sans [font-weight:var(--type-footnote-font-weight)] [&amp;_p]:text-muted-foreground"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6"><em><strong>Close of Trading Times</strong></em>. Although both the NAV and the daily market price of the Fund are generally calculated based on prices at the closing time of the exchange (generally 4:00 p.m. eastern time), slight differences in this timing may cause discrepancies.<br/><br/><em><strong>Time of Last Trade</strong></em><strong>.</strong> Trading of Global X funds generally takes place during normal trading hours (9:30 a.m. to 4:00 p.m. eastern time). However, it is important to note that the last trade - from which the closing price is determined - may not occur at exactly 4:00 p.m. eastern time. Therefore, changing market sentiment during the time difference may cause the NAV to deviate from the closing price.<br/><br/><em><strong>International Holdings</strong></em>. The premiums and discounts for funds with significant holdings in international markets may be less accurate due to the different closing times of various international markets. Because the Funds trade during U.S. market hours while the underlying securities may not, the time lapse between the markets can result in differences between the NAV and the trading price.</p></div></div></div></div><script>$RS("S:38","P:38")</script><div hidden id="S:39"><div class="max-w-6xl text-(length:--type-footnote-font-size) leading-(--type-footnote-line-height) text-muted-foreground font-sans font-(--type-footnote-font-weight) [&amp;_p]:text-muted-foreground"><h6 class="font-heading text-base font-medium leading-6 text-background not-last:mb-6">The Hypothetical Growth of $10,000 chart reflects a hypothetical $10,000 investment at the first trading date of the period shown and assumes reinvestment of dividends and capital gains. Fund expenses, including management fees and other expenses were deducted.</h6><h6 class="font-heading text-base font-medium leading-6 text-background not-last:mb-6"><em><strong>The performance quoted represents past performance and does not guarantee future results. Investment return and principal value of an investment will fluctuate so that an investor&#x27;s shares, when sold or redeemed, may be worth more or less than the original cost. Current performance may be lower or higher than the performance quoted, and numbers may reflect small variances due to rounding. Standardized performance and performance data current to the most recent month end may be found in the Performance section.</strong></em></h6></div></div><script>$RS("S:39","P:39")</script><div hidden [&amp;:has([role=checkbox])]:pr-0 h-14 py-2 text-right text-muted-foreground">$0.36</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div><script>$RS("S:32","P:32")</script><div hidden id="S:33"><ol class="flex max-w-6xl flex-col gap-4 text-xs text-muted-foreground"><li class="[&amp;_p]:inline"><span class="mr-1">1</span><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The distribution as a percentage an investor would have received if they had held the fund over the last twelve months, assuming the most recent NAV. The Trailing 12-Month Distribution (%) is calculated by summing any income, capital gains and return of capital distributions over the past twelve months and dividing by the sum of the most recent NAV and any capital gain distributions made over the same period. The distribution is estimated to include a return of capital. For information on the breakdown of the distributions, please see the <a data-user-action="fund-info-download-click" data-document-type="Form 19a" data-fund-ticker="XYLD" data-fund-themes="Income, Covered Call" data-click-location="distributions" href="https://assets.ctfassets.net/9gigg57dwmx9/5Xu3w9H0qtwWla7onav2on/d0f802748bb7d458e069cb71578adeb4/XYLD_Form-19a_09242026.docx" class="cursor-pointer text-primary hover:text-background border-b border-primary hover:border-foreground no-underline" ignore-external-icon="true">19a Notice</a>. These do not imply rates for any future distributions.</p></li><li class="[&amp;_p]:inline"><span class="mr-1">2</span><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The annual rate an investor would receive if the most recent fund distribution remained the same going forward. The rate represents a single distribution from the fund and does not represent total return of the fund. The distribution rate is calculated by annualizing the most recent distribution and dividing by the most recent fund NAV. The distribution is estimated to include a return of capital. For information on the breakdown of the distributions, please see the <a data-user-action="fund-info-download-click" data-document-type="Form 19a" data-fund-ticker="XYLD" data-fund-themes="Income, Covered Call" data-click-location="distributions" href="https://assets.ctfassets.net/9gigg57dwmx9/5Xu3w9H0qtwWla7onav2on/d0f802748bb7d458e069cb71578adeb4/XYLD_Form-19a_09242026.docx" class="cursor-pointer text-primary hover:text-background border-b border-primary hover:border-foreground no-underline" ignore-external-icon="true">19a Notice</a>. These do not imply rates for any future distributions.</p></li></ol></div><script>$RS("S:33","P:33")</script><div hidden id="S:36"><p class="text-background not-last:mb-6">The performance data quoted represents past performance. Past performance does not guarantee future results. The investment return and principal value of an investment will fluctuate so that an investor&#x27;s shares, when sold or redeemed, may be worth more or less than their original cost and current performance may be lower or higher than the performance quoted. A portion of the distribution is estimated to include a return of capital. For information on the breakdown of the distributions, please see the Tax Documents. These do not imply rates for any future distributions.</p></div><script>$RS("S:36","P:36")</script><div hidden id="S:2e">History</div><script>$RS("S:2e","P:2e")</script><div hidden id="S:2f">As The vertical axis of the chart shows the premium or discount of the daily market price as a percentage of the NAV. The horizontal axis shows each trading day in the time period, and each data point in the chart represents the Premium/Discount on that day. The data presented in the chart and table above represent past performance and cannot be used to predict future results.\"]]}]\ndd:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"text-background not-last:mb-6\",\"children\":[[\"The primary explanation is that timing discrepancies can arise between the NAV and the trading price of the Fund. Since shares of the Fund trade on the open market, prices are affected by the constant flow of information received by investors, corporations and financial institutions. Depending on how this changing information affects investor sentiment, shares of the Fund may deviate slightly from the value of the Fund's underlying assets. The NAV of the Fund is only calculated once a day (normally at 4:00 p.m. eastern time). As a result, shareholders may pay more than NAV when they buy Fund shares and receive less than NAV when they sell those shares, because shares are purchased and sold at current market prices. However, due to the creation and redemption process that is unique to ETFs, market makers are able to minimize these deviations from NAV by taking advantage of arbitrage opportunities.\"]]}]\nde:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"text-background not-last:mb-6\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"em\",\"0\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":[\"Close of Trading Times\"]}]}],[\". Although both the NAV and the daily market price of the Fund are generally calculated based on prices at the closing time of the exchange (generally 4:00 p.m. eastern time), slight differences in this timing may cause discrepancies.\",[\"$\",\"br\",null,{}],\"\",[\"$\",\"br\",null,{}],\"\"],[\"$\",\"em\",\"2\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":[\"Time of Last Trade\"]}]}],[\"$\",\"strong\",\"3\",{\"children\":[\".\"]}],[\" Trading of Global X funds generally takes place during normal trading hours (9:30 a.m. to 4:00 p.m. eastern time). However, it is important to note that the last trade - from which the closing price is determined - may not occur at exactly 4:00 p.m. eastern time. Therefore, changing market sentiment during the time difference may cause the NAV to deviate from the closing price.\",[\"$\",\"br\",null,{}],\"\",[\"$\",\"br\",null,{}],\"\"],[\"$\",\"em\",\"5\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":[\"International Holdings\"]}]}],[\". The premiums and discounts for funds with significant holdings in international markets may be less accurate due to the different closing times of various international markets.
